4. The super Littlewood–Richardson rule

In this section, we apply the super-RSK correspondence in order to give a combinatorial version of the super Littlewood–Richardson rule on super Schur functions over a finite signed alphabet.
Finally, we introduce the notion of super Littlewood–Richardson skew tableaux and we give another version of the super Littlewood–Richardson rule.

In the sequel, we will assume that
\Si
is finite.

4.1. Super Littlewood–Richardson coefficients

Let
\lambda
,
\mu
and
\nu
be in
\mathcal{P}
such that
\nu/\lambda
is a skew shape.
We want to compute the number of ways a given super tableau
t
in
\YoungT(\Si,\nu)
can be written as the product of a super tableau
t^{\prime}
in
\YoungT(\Si,\lambda)
and a super tableau
t^{\prime\prime}
in
\YoungT(\Si,\mu)
.

For any super tableau
t
in
\YoungT(\Si,\nu)
, we set

\YoungT(\Si,\lambda,\mu,\dashv t)\;:=\;\big\{(t^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime})\in\YoungT(\Si,\lambda)\times\YoungT(\Si,\mu)\;\big|\;t=t^{\prime}\star_{\YoungT(\Si)}t^{\prime\prime}\big\},

and we call the integer

c_{\lambda,\mu}^{\nu}\;:=\;\#\YoungT(\Si,\lambda,\mu,\dashv t)

the
super Littlewood–Richardson coefficient
.

Using the super jeu de taquin and as discussed in
2.4.4
, the integer
c_{\lambda,\mu}^{\nu}
is equal to the number of super skew tableaux of the following form:

[t^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime}]\;=\;

t^{\prime\prime}

t^{\prime}

whose rectification is
t
.
We will prove that the integer
c_{\lambda,\mu}^{\nu}
is also equal to the number of super skew tableaux in
\SkewT(\Si,\nu/\lambda)
whose rectification is a given super tableau of shape
\mu
.

For any
T
in
\YoungT(\Si,\mu)
, we set

\SkewT(\Si,\nu/\lambda,\dashv T)\;:=\;\big\{S\in\SkewT(\Si,\nu/\lambda)\;\big|\;\Rec(S)=T\big\}.

We prove in Theorem
4.2.1
that there is a canonical one-to-one correspondence between the sets
\YoungT(\Si,\lambda,\mu,\dashv t)
and
\SkewT(\Si,\nu/\lambda,\dashv T)
. We deduce that the coefficient
c_{\lambda,\mu}^{\nu}
does not depend on
t
and
T
, and depends only on
\lambda
,
\mu
and
\nu
. Note that the later property is proved in
[
13
]
using an interpretation of the super jeu de taquin in terms of Fomin’s growth diagrams.

4.2. Super Littlewood–Richardson rule

We denote by
R_{\Si}
the
\mathbb{Z}
-algebra constructed from the super plactic monoid
\mathbf{P}(\Si)
whose linear generators are the monomials in
\mathbf{P}(\Si)
.
This algebra is an associative and unitary ring that is not commutative. A generic element in
R_{\Si}
is realized by a formal sum of super plactic classes with coefficients from
\mathbb{Z}
.
Following Property
2.3.1
, a typical element in
R_{\Si}
is a formal sum of super tableaux.
A canonical homomorphism from
R_{\Si}
onto the ring of polynomials
\mathbb{Z}[X]
is obtained by taking each super tableau
t
to its monomial
x^{t}
, where
x^{t}
is the product of the variables
x_{i}
, each occurring as many times in
x^{t}
as
i
occurs in
t
. For instance, the following monomial

x^{\mathbf{T}(w)}=x_{1}^{3}x_{2}^{2}x_{3}^{2}x_{4}^{4}x_{5}^{3}x_{6}^{2}\quad(\text{resp}.\penalty\ x^{\mathbf{Q}(w)}=x_{1}^{1}x_{2}^{3}x_{3}^{3}x_{4}^{3}x_{5}^{3}x_{6}^{3})

corresponds to
\mathbf{T}(w)
(resp.
\mathbf{Q}(w)
) computed in Example
3.1.6
. Moreover, the following monomial

x^{\mathbf{T}(w)}=x_{1}^{2}x_{2}^{2}x_{3}^{2}x_{4}\quad(\text{resp}.\penalty\ x^{\mathbf{Q}(w)}=x_{1}^{2}x_{2}^{2}x_{3}^{2}x_{4}^{2})

corresponds to the super tableau
\mathbf{T}(w)
(resp.
\mathbf{Q}(w)
) computed in Example
3.1.7
.

We define
S_{\lambda}
(resp.
S_{\lambda/\mu}
) in
R_{\Si}
to be the sum of all super tableaux (resp. super skew tableaux) of shape
\lambda
(resp.
\lambda/\mu
) and entries in
\Si
, with
\lambda\in\mathcal{P}
(resp.
\lambda/\mu
is a skew shape).
By taking the image of
S_{\lambda}
(resp.
S_{\lambda}/\mu
) in
\mathbb{Z}[X]
, we obtain the so-called
super Schur function
(resp.
super skew Schur function
)
s_{\lambda}(X)
(resp.
s_{\lambda/\mu}(X)
).

Theorem 4.2.1

(The super Littlewood–Richardson rule)
.

Let
\lambda
,
\mu
and
\nu
be partitions in
\mathcal{P}
such that
\nu/\lambda
is a skew shape.
The following identities

S_{\lambda}S_{\mu}\;=\;\underset{\nu}{\sum}c_{\lambda,\mu}^{\nu}S_{\nu}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad S_{\nu/\lambda}\;=\;\underset{\mu}{\sum}c_{\lambda,\mu}^{\nu}S_{\mu}

(10)

hold in
R_{\Si}
.

Proof.

Prove that for any
t
in
\YoungT(\Si,\nu)
and any
T
in
\YoungT(\Si,\mu)
, there is a canonical one-to-one correspondence between the sets
\YoungT(\Si,\lambda,\mu,\dashv t)
and
\SkewT(\Si,\nu/\lambda,\dashv T)
.
Start with
(t^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime})
in
\YoungT(\Si,\lambda,\mu,\dashv t)
and let

\begin{pmatrix}x_{1}&\ldots&x_{k}\\
y_{1}&\ldots&y_{k}\end{pmatrix}

be the signed two-rowed array on
\Si
corresponding to the couple
(t^{\prime\prime},T)
.
By inserting the letters
y_{i}
, for
i=1,\ldots,k
, into the super tableau
t^{\prime}
, we form a super skew tableau
S
by successively placing the letter
x_{i}
, for
i=1,\ldots,k
, into the new boxes starting with an empty Young diagram of the same shape as
t^{\prime}
.
Since the super tableau
t=t^{\prime}\star_{\YoungT(\Si)}t^{\prime\prime}
has shape
\nu
, then the super skew tableau
S
has shape
\nu/\lambda
and we deduce by Proposition
3.3.1
that
S
belongs to
\SkewT(\Si,\nu/\lambda,\dashv T)
.

Conversely, start with a super skew tableau
S
in
\SkewT(\Si,\nu/\lambda,\dashv T)
, and
let
T^{\prime}
be in
\YoungT(\Si^{<},\lambda)
where
\Si^{<}
is a signed alphabet whose elements are all smaller than the ones of
\Si
.
Let
T^{\prime\prime}
be the super tableau in
\YoungT(\Si^{<}\cup\Si,\nu)
that contains the super tableau
T^{\prime}
such that when we remove
T^{\prime}
from
T^{\prime\prime}
we obtain the super skew tableau
S
. By Theorem
3.1.5
, the couple
(t,T^{\prime\prime})
corresponds to a unique signed two-rowed array of the following form
\begin{pmatrix}x^{\prime}_{1}&\ldots&x^{\prime}_{l}&x_{1}&\ldots&x_{k}\\
y^{\prime}_{1}&\ldots&y^{\prime}_{l}&y_{1}&\ldots&y_{k}\end{pmatrix}
.
Then the signed two rowed array
\begin{pmatrix}x^{\prime}_{1}&\ldots&x^{\prime}_{l}\\
y^{\prime}_{1}&\ldots&y^{\prime}_{l}\end{pmatrix}
corresponds to the couple
(t^{\prime},T^{\prime})
, and following Proposition
3.3.1
the signed two-rowed array
\begin{pmatrix}x_{1}&\ldots&x_{k}\\
y_{1}&\ldots&y_{k}\end{pmatrix}
corresponds to the couple
(t^{\prime\prime},T)
, for some super tableaux
t^{\prime}
in
\YoungT(\Si,\lambda)
and
t^{\prime\prime}
in
\YoungT(\Si,\mu)
, such that
t^{\prime}\star_{\YoungT(\Si)}t^{\prime\prime}=t
. Hence, we obtain a pair
(t^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime})
in
\YoungT(\Si,\lambda,\mu,\dashv t)
. We deduce that the cardinal number of the set
\SkewT(\Si,\nu/\lambda,\dashv T)
is equal to the coefficient
c_{\lambda,\mu}^{\nu}
, showing the claim.
∎

Since neither
\YoungT(\Si,\lambda,\mu,\dashv t)
nor
\SkewT(\Si,\nu/\lambda,\dashv T)
in the correspondence of the proof of Theorem
4.2.1
depends on the contents of the super tableaux used to define the other, we deduce the following result:

Corollary 4.2.2
.

The cardinal number
c_{\lambda,\mu}^{\nu}
of the sets
\YoungT(\Si,\lambda,\mu,\dashv t)
and
\SkewT(\Si,\nu/\lambda,\dashv T)
is independent of choice of
t
and
T
and depends only on
\lambda
,
\mu
and
\nu
.

4.3. Super Littlewood–Richardson skew tableaux

We introduce in this final part the notion of super Littlewood–Richardson skew tableaux and we give a new combinatorial description of the super Littlewood–Richardson coefficient.
We suppose in the sequel that

\Si\;=\;\{\overline{m}<\ldots<\overline{1}<1<\ldots<n\}

with
\Si_{1}=\{1<\ldots<n\}
and
\Si_{0}=\{\overline{m}<\ldots<\overline{1}\}
.
For any
w
in
\Si^{\ast}
, we denote by
|w|_{i}
the number of times the element
i
of
\Si
appears in
w
, and by
\underline{|w|}
the number of elements of
\Si_{1}
appearing in
w
such that each element is counted only once.
The
weight map
is the map

\wt:\Si^{\ast}\to\big(\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\cup\{0\}\big)^{m+n}

defined by
\wt(w)=(|w|_{\overline{m}},\ldots,|w|_{\overline{1}},|w|_{1},\ldots,|w|_{n})
, for all
w
in
\Si^{\ast}
. We call
\wt(\mathbf{R}(S))
the
weight
of a super skew tableau
S
over
\Si
.

4.3.1. Super Yamanouchi words

A word
w
over
\Si
is a
super Yamanouchi word
if it satisfies the following two conditions:

i)

for every right subword
w^{\prime}
of
w
, the following property holds:

|w^{\prime}|_{\overline{m}}\geqslant\ldots\geqslant|w^{\prime}|_{\overline{2}}\geqslant|w^{\prime}|_{\overline{1}}\geqslant\underline{|w^{\prime}|},

ii)

for every left subword
w^{\prime}
of
w
, the following property holds:

|w^{\prime}|_{1}\geqslant|w^{\prime}|_{2}\geqslant\ldots\geqslant|w^{\prime}|_{n}.

When
\Si=\Si_{0}
, we recover the notion of
Yamanouchi words
that describes the elements of highest weights for the crystal structure of the general Lie algebra of type A,
[
23
]
.

For instance, suppose
\Si=\{\overline{2}<\overline{1}<1<2\}
. The word
12\overline{2}\;\overline{1}\;\overline{1}\;\overline{2}\;\overline{2}
is a super Yamanouchi word over
\Si
. However, the word
w=\overline{1}1\overline{2}2\overline{1}\;\overline{2}\;\overline{2}
is not a super Yamanouchi word over
\Si
because for its right subword
w^{\prime}=1\overline{2}2\overline{1}\;\overline{2}\;\overline{2}
, we have
|w^{\prime}|_{\overline{1}}<\underline{|w^{\prime}|}
.

Lemma 4.3.2
.

Let
w
and
v
be words over
\Si
that are super plactic equivalent. Then
w
is a super Yamanouchi word if and only if
v
is a super Yamanouchi word.

Proof.

Suppose first that
w=uxzyu^{\prime}
and
v=uzxyu^{\prime}
for all
u,u^{\prime}
in
\Si^{\ast}
such that
x\leqslant y\leqslant z
in
\Si
with
x=y
only if
||y||=0
and
y=z
only if
||y||=1
.
If
x<y<z
, there is no changes in the numbers of consecutive elements of
\Si_{0}
(resp.
\Si_{1}
) in each right (resp. left) subword of
w
and
v
.
If
x=\overline{1}
and
y=z=1
or
x=y=\overline{1}
and
z=1
such that
w
is a super Yamanouchi word. Then
|u^{\prime}|_{\overline{1}}\geqslant\underline{|u^{\prime}|}
, and hence
v
is a super Yamanouchi word. Similarly, we show that if
v
is a super Yamanouchi word, then
w
is so.
If
x=y=k\in\Si_{0}
and
z=k+1\in\Si_{0}
such that
w
is a super Yamanouchi word. Then by definition, we have
|u^{\prime}|_{k}\geqslant|u^{\prime}|_{k+1}
, showing that
zxyu^{\prime}
is a super Yamanouchi word, and then
v
is so. Similarly, we show that if
v
is a super Yamanouchi word, then
w
is so. If
x=k\in\Si_{1}
and
y=z=k+1\in\Si_{1}
such that
w
is a super Yamanouchi word. Then the following property
|u|_{k}>|u|_{k+1}
holds, showing that
uzxy
is a super Yamanouchi word, and hence
v
is so. Similarly, we show that if
v
is a super Yamanouchi word, then
w
is so.

Suppose now that
w=uyxzu^{\prime}
and
v=uyzxu^{\prime}
for all
u,u^{\prime}
in
\Si^{\ast}
such that
x\leqslant y\leqslant z
in
\Si
with
x=y
only if
||y||=1
and
y=z
only if
||y||=0
. If
x<y<z
, there is no changes in the numbers of consecutive elements of
\Si_{0}
(resp.
\Si_{1}
) in each right (resp. left) subword of
w
and
v
. Suppose
x=y=k\in\Si_{1}
and
z=k+1\in\Si_{1}
such that
w
is a super Yamanouchi word. Then by definition, we have
|u|_{k}>|u|_{k+1}
, showing that
uyzx
is a super Yamanouchi word, and hence
v
is so. Similarly, we show that if
v
is a super Yamanouchi word, then
w
is so. Suppose now that
x=k\in\Si_{0}
and
y=z=k+1\in\Si_{0}
such that
w
is a super Yamanouchi word. Then by definition, the following property
|u^{\prime}|_{k}>|u^{\prime}|_{k+1}
holds, showing that
yzxu^{\prime}
is a super Yamanouchi word, and then
v
is so. Similarly, we show that if
v
is a super Yamanouchi word, then
w
is so.
∎

4.3.3. Super Littlewood–Richardson skew tableaux

We say that a super skew tableau
S
over
\Si
is a
super Littlewood–Richardson skew tableau
if its reading word
\mathbf{R}(S)
is a super Yamanouchi word.
For instance, the following super tableau over
\Si=\{\overline{2}<\overline{1}<1<2\}
is a super Littlewood–Richardson skew tableau of shape
(7,5,3,3,2,1,1)/(4,3,2,2,1)
and of weight
(3,2,3,2)
:

\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern height=10.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-10.39995pt\hrule width=10.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 10.00002pt\hrule width=10.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to10.39995pt{\vss\hbox to10.00002pt{\hss$\mbox{\footnotesize$1$}$\hss}\vss}\kern-10.39995pt\vrule height=10.39995pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 10.00002pt\vrule height=10.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-10.39995pt\hrule width=10.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 10.00002pt\hrule width=10.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}\crcr}}\kern 2134.35667pt}.

For any partition
\mu
in
\mathcal{P}
of height
k
, we define the
(n+m)
-uplet
\check{\mu}:=(\mu_{1},\widetilde{\mu_{2}})

where
\mu_{1}
is the partition formed by the first
m
parts of
\mu
and
\mu_{2}
is the partition formed by its last
k-m
parts, such that if
m\leqslant k<m+n
then the last
(m+n)-k
parts of
\mu_{2}
are zero and if
k<m
then the last
m-k
parts of
\mu_{1}
are zero and all the parts of
\mu_{2}
are zero. For instance, suppose
\Si=\{\overline{2}<\overline{1}<1<2\}
and
\mu=(3,2,2,2,1)
. Then
\check{\mu}=(3,2,3,2)
with
\mu_{1}=(3,2)
,
\mu_{2}=(2,2,1)
and
\widetilde{\mu_{2}}=(3,2)
.

Let
\mu
be a partition.
Denote by
T(\mu)
the super tableau of shape
\mu
whose first row contains only
\overline{m}
, the second row contains only
\overline{m+1}
, and so on row by row, until the
m
-th row that contains only
\overline{1}
, and starting from the nonempty
(m+1)
-th box of each column, the corresponding
i
-th column contains only
i
.
Then, the super tableau
T(\mu)
can be divided into two super tableaux: the first one of shape
\mu_{1}
that contains only the elements of
\Si_{0}
and the second one of shape
\mu_{2}
that contains only the elements of
\Si_{1}
, such that the following equality holds:

\wt(\mathbf{R}(T(\mu)))\;=\;(\mu_{1},\widetilde{\mu_{2}})\;=\;\check{\mu}.

For instance, suppose
\Si=\{\overline{2}<\overline{1}<1<2\}
and
\mu=(3,2,2,2,1)
. Then we have

T(\mu)\;=\;

\overline{2}

\overline{2}

\overline{1}

\overline{1}

1

2

1

2

1

\text{ with }\penalty\ \mu_{1}=(3,2),\penalty\ \mu_{2}=(2,2,1)\text{ and }\penalty\ \check{\mu}=(3,2,3,2)=\wt(\mathbf{R}(T(\mu))).

It is clear from the definition of
T(\mu)
, that it is the only super tableau of shape
\mu
whose reading is a super Yamanouchi word. Hence, following Lemma
4.3.2
, we deduce the following result:

Lemma 4.3.4
.

Let
\mu
be a partition in
\mathcal{P}
. A super skew tableau over
\Si
is a super Littlewood–Richardson skew tableau of weight
\check{\mu}
if and only if its rectification is the super tableau
T(\mu)
.

As a consequence, following Theorem
4.2.1
, we deduce the following result:

Theorem 4.3.5
.

Let
\lambda
,
\mu
and
\nu
be partitions in
\mathcal{P}
such that
\nu/\lambda
is a skew shape.
The super Littlewood–Richardson coefficient
c_{\lambda,\mu}^{\nu}
is equal to the number of super Littlewood–Richardson skew tableaux of shape
\nu/\lambda
and of weight
\check{\mu}
.

Example 4.3.6
.

Suppose
\Si=\{\overline{2}<\overline{1}<1<2\}
,
\nu=(5,4,3,2)
and
\lambda=(3,3,1)
.
The super Littlewood–Richardson skew tableaux of shape
(5,4,3,2)/(3,3,1)
are the following:

Following Theorem
4.3.5
, we have:

i)

c_{\lambda,\mu}^{\nu}=1
, for
\mu=(5,2)
,
\mu=(5,1,1)
,
\mu=(3,3,1)
,

ii)

c_{\lambda,\mu}^{\nu}=2
, for
\mu=(4,3)
,
\mu=(4,2,1)
,
\mu=(3,2,2)
,

iii)

c_{\lambda,\mu}^{\nu}=0
, for other
\mu
.

Hence, we obtain the following decomposition in
R_{\Si}
:

S_{(5,4,3,2)/(3,3,1)}\;=\;S_{(5,2)}+S_{(5,1,1)}+2S_{(4,3)}+2S_{(4,2,1)}+2S_{(3,2,2)}+S_{(3,3,1)}.

Note finally that the readings of the
genuine highest weight super tableaux
introduced in
[
1
]
as highest weight vectors for the crystal graph for the representations of the general linear Lie superalgebra of type A are super Yamanouchi words. A future work would be to investigate the combinatorial properties of the representations of the general linear Lie superalgebra
\mathfrak{gl}_{m,n}
using the constructions developed in this article.
